The transition is too abrupt and requires more explanation and development. It feels unrealistic that Hiroshi is able to quickly harness his mystical abilities and become a skilled policeman without proper training or experience.\nAdditionally, the plot suffers from an overload of characters with unique gifts. While diversity in the cast can be a strength, the inclusion of Shinto priests, exorcists, and alchemists alongside Hiroshi’s already complex storyline makes it difficult to fully explore each character’s depth and development. This results in a lack of emotional connection and leaves many characters feeling underdeveloped and neglected.\nFurthermore, the overarching conspiracy that threatens the fabric of reality is clichéd and lacks originality. The malevolent sorcerer seeking to tap into the ancient Egyptian powers for his twisted ambitions feels like a tired trope that has been seen in many other anime series. A more unique and original villain would have added depth and intrigue to the story.\nLastly, the exploration of ancient Egyptian mythology feels superficial and underutilized. While the concept of Hiroshi’s connection to the Egyptian gods is intriguing, it is not fully explored and integrated into the story. The potential for rich storytelling and world-building is wasted, leaving the audience wanting more depth and substance.\nOverall, “The son of my mother’s friend mastered the Egyptian power and became a Japanese policeman” fails to fully develop its intriguing premise and struggles to balance its complex plot and character dynamics.
